Contains §4 (the areas and their rows). The rest: the head, §1, §2, §3, §5 and §6 (its opening and Areas 1-3): `walk-coverage-T51.md`; §6 Areas 4-10: `walk-coverage-T51-part2.md`; §6 Areas 11-12 and the Overlay (with its six lists): `walk-coverage-T51-part3.md`.

## 4. The areas and their rows

### Area 1 · Install, claim, set up the room

**The sitting.** One walker, one new empty room, in this order. (1) Start from the installer's own address, because civicsmith.org carries no link to it (INSTALLER_ADDRESS unset, K4784); read the install pages in order (Get ready, start, Cloudflare permission, Check my account, progress, done) and the existing-copy, update and removal pages. Run the whole install and then an update offline in Miniflare first, on its fake Cloudflare account, and write down which environment each act ran in: the offline run does not exercise the three-act binding order or the closing re-PUT that keeps passwords and the Durable Object (DI:11, 156), and it can only be run after release 0.87.0, because G90 did not ship in redeploy 2496ff995b (P:400, 389). Run the old-copy update (a copy from before 0.71.0) and the lost-password recovery on that offline copy, never in the room the later areas use. Then, if BOB allows, once on a scratch Cloudflare account. (2) The room is re-created and claimed (G83): the claim spends ADMIN_TOKEN and may be a step only BOB can do, so the walker records who did it and walks every claim act that is still open. Then the setup parts in order: claim, handle, recovery codes shown once, the "who controls this copy" block and the hosting holder, team name, website file and "Verify the website" (R08), logo, languages, places, profiles and time zone, offices, assistant and AI choices, court notice, the optional "Say who your team is" side trip, and last the first invitation (memberadd). That invitation is the member account every later area needs; its join, sign-in and welcome belong to other rows (R04 to R07, R10 to R12, taken by other writers). A second administrator is also needed here as a fixture (the same fixture as R11): the export notice to every administrator, the gauge's three viewer classes and the governor refusal all need one. (3) Back in the room as an administrator and as the member: team identity (R08), standards (R09), Settings list, glossary, explanations, time zone, translations by hand (needs a second granted speaker). (4) Last, as a stranger in a browser with no session: the front door and the site pages (R01), which by then show the team's name and logo and, in an empty room, no published cases (the listing with cases belongs to area 9). Updates (R14) is read in the room (no newer release, so only the health and the "up to date" state) and walked in full on the offline copy with a newer signed release.

*Rows placed in this area when the areas were reconciled (merged or moved here, or recovered proposals), taken in the sitting where their prerequisites stand:* R02, R03, R110, R111, R112, R113, R114, R115, R116, R117, R118, R119, R120.

### Area 2 · The team's people and accounts

**The sitting.** One walker, in the new empty room that area 1 leaves behind (claimed, the founder signed in, the team named, the first member invited or ready to be). Start as the founder with R04 (sign-in states, a wrong password, the two kinds of device, the password-again step); then R10 (the roster: invite the first member with a cover, capabilities and declared expertise; copy the link; switch on the website key and the reusable join link), because nothing else here can be walked without a second person. Take R06 next, as that invitee in a second browser (member account 1), then once more through the reusable join link as a second invitee (member account 2); the welcome wizard's question step has no question to show in the empty room, so record what it shows and re-run it after the question area. Now return to R04 with both accounts in one browser (the second must see none of the first's values; time the 30-minute lock), then R12 and R05 as founder and as member (key, language, theme, pairing, information level, expertise, password change, sign out on this device and everywhere, which ends the member's other session). R07 follows because it spends codes and ends sessions: the recovery page, a forgotten password by code, an administrator's reset link for a member without codes. R11 needs a fixture (a second administrator, then a third with every administrator's endorsement, a removal vote, a key a member registered for herself) and a member who can be proposed. R13 splits: the Security page reading, "Add another checker" and its test and removal can be done now; the file-side acts (safe view, open the original, deeper check, a hold released by two members) need captured files from the intake areas, so they come last, as the member too. R15 splits: write, adopt and read the lens now (administrator); the debt, the project lens and the ceremony's Bias step need a project, a run or a case, so they wait for the later areas; settling debt by re-run needs the assistant (after the alpha). R17 closes the area (connect page skipped or "Not now", the not-ready state, the Ask page as search) and is best re-read once documents exist. Split with area 1: install, claim and the first invitation, team identity (R08), standards (R09), updates (R14), the Settings list and More tab, wizards as a library, interface translation, and the lost-founder-password route belong there; this area takes the people, their accounts and their sessions. Member accounts are needed for R04, R05, R06, R07, R10 (to use the link and key), R11, R12, R13 (file side) and R17.

*Rows placed in this area when the areas were reconciled (merged or moved here, or recovered proposals), taken in the sitting where their prerequisites stand:* R121, R122, R123, R124, R125, R126.

### Area 3 · Projects and the daily rhythm

**The sitting.** One walker, in the new empty room that area 1 (and the area after it that sets the room up) left behind: the founder (administrator) is signed in, the team has a name and bar, and at least one member account exists who joined with the default (may contribute, may not start projects) plus a second member for the rows that need one (shared resolution, "Ask for a check" by expertise, the `out_of_view` outsider, a mute the other account still sees). Order: (1) **Home, then Queue, first as the founder on the empty room, then as the freshly joined member** (R20, R21, R22): read the new-team Home ("Nothing is here yet", numbered first steps with "Name your team", the "What happened?" doors, the "Getting into a project" list for the member who may not start projects), the empty Queue ("Nothing is waiting on you" / "You are up to date"), each Home door once, and the member's invitation as one Queue item. Nothing else is in the Queue yet. (2) **Start a project as the founder** (R23): both visibility choices offered and neither preselected, a duplicate and a Unicode-equivalent name refused, an unchosen start refused; then as the member read the Projects list (no "Start a project", the administrator named) and the directory of findable projects, with a hidden one absent. (3) **The project page** (R24) on the empty project: stage, what each stage needs, visibility, bar, stake, the doors (Calculation, Matter, Action, Plan), the owner-only settings; read it also as the member who is not in it (existence only) and as an invited member who has not joined (skeleton only). (4) **Membership both ways** (R25): invite the member; she joins; a second member requests to join a findable project, the owner answers from Needs you / Queue (grant invites, she still has to join), withdraw a request; make an owner (sole owner alone, then every owner must agree; test at exactly two owners), stand down, leave (last owner refused), fork; rescue needs a fixture (a project whose every owner is inactive, made inactive from the roster of area 1) and an administrator who is not an owner. The member filing into a project she is not in (G74) is read here as a refusal on the Add screen; the filing itself (captureupload, promote) belongs to the documents area, which this sitting cannot yet do. (5) **Objective** (R26) on the project: set the condition, read the gaps, and record exactly which aspiration and goal controls exist. (6) **Themes** (R27): find the door by pressing, declare a theme with its test; placing a document or passage and withdrawing a hunch need documents, so they are finished after the documents area. (7) **Dashboards** (R28): open `dash-project` and `dash-action` from Home's doors; the next-steps lists are thin until later areas leave material. **Return passes:** Home and Queue are walked again at the end of areas 6, 10 and 11, because their items exist only after later areas. The pass at the end of area 6 must find the version items ("newer version" with its grade, adopt or keep; G66, G89, G65), the contradiction ("tension") candidates, the vouch band, the held and fetch-done and ingest-done notices of documents and money work, and the "Hunch" items; the pass at the end of area 10 must find the tension-after-publication items for published cases, the review-copy and edition notices (including scheduled-edition notices) and the docket To-dos; the pass at the end of area 11 must find the Action-layer items (checkpoint due, proposed escalation stage, a reminder the member asked for, an Action's deadline passed with "no reply recorded", the litigation-hold reminder, the unsent records request with "Pick up where you left off") and the same Home counts equal to the Queue rows. Member account needed for R20, R21 (second arm), R22, R23 (non-member read), R24 (outsider and invited views), R25, R26 (member scope), R27 (handle versus member id).

### Area 4 · Bring documents in (two sittings, 4a and 4b)

**The sitting.** One walker takes this area in a new empty room that area 1 has already opened (administrator signed in, the member account joined, hosting and the team's place set) and area 2 has furnished (at least one project the member belongs to and one project she does not, so the filing refusal of G74 can be provoked; a second project member for the second approval in R100). The member account is needed in R31, R32, R33 (typed-page check), R36 (a member adds), the held-list rows, R39 (Inbox), R40 (named and non-named reads), R41 (leads shared into a project) and R100; a stranger with no account (the public frame) is needed for the doorbell in R39 and the knocker's consent in R40. Fixtures to have ready before the first press: a public page that renders (dena, Wikipedia), a slow page (acadia), a client-rendered shell, a page with a missing stylesheet, a site that refuses the plane, a 15.3-15.9 MB PDF address with and without Content-Length (govinfo), a 41.5 MB file, PDFs of 10 MB, 23.2 MB, 20 pages and the 1,354-page volume 0111, a 30-page and a 60-page scan, a phone photo, a baseline-JPEG scan and a progressive-JPEG scan, text of 12 MB and 24 MiB, CSVs of 1 MB, 9 MB and 17 MiB (comma, tab, semicolon, pipe, UTF-16, latin-1, with and without a declared type), the 18,098-row table, workbooks of about 20,000, 60,000 and 221,376 cells, an ODS declaring over 8 MiB, one each of docx, pptx, odt, odp, a legacy .doc, RTF, an email, PNG (also interlaced), JPEG, and a ZIP of 30 documents with a locked entry. Order, as a founder's investigation runs: (1) start with R30 (capture by address), because it makes the first documents and the first Queue notice, and press the long reads early; (2) R31 (upload) next, pressing the 12 MB text, the 24 MiB text, the 23 MB PDF, the 17 MiB CSV and the scan first so their reads, which take up to about 30 minutes each, run while the walker does the short rows; poll the owed-work ledger after each large intake (the ledger row belongs to the area that owns it); (3) R32 (observation), then the corroboration row R31 (same bytes by two routes, so it needs R30 and R31 done); (4) read what the intake made: R34 (PDF), R33 (scans, typed page, second-member check; the typed-page check needs the member account and hands its row to R85 in the question area), R35 (text and CSV), R36 (workbooks), R37 (the other office kinds and the ZIP), then the held-list and document-page rows R46 to R46 (selection, set-aside while a question waits, links and record of a copy, content questions), which read documents the earlier rows left behind; the set-aside row needs a question from area 8's opening acts, so do it in the 4b half (below) or leave it for the end; (5) the doorbell and Inbox R39 (stranger, then member pulling a text, a PDF and a CSV into the room), then R40 (a source moved from unknown to known; consent) which needs a knock from R39; (6) R41 and R135 to R137 (watches, leads, a source that changes, the archive route), which need captured addresses from R30, need time to tick (give them at least one hourly tick), and need a watched address that can be changed or taken down; (7) R100 last, because the reply to a records request arrives as a document: the member files the request, then the reply is brought in by upload, doorbell or address and recorded on the action; the second approval needs the second project member, the Oakland template needs the template process, and the part of R39 that depends on a litigation hold waits for the hold made in the action area. **Proposed split:** the sitting is too long for one walker (many fixtures, reads of up to 30 minutes, two accounts, hourly ticks, a stranger). Take 4a = R30, R31, R32, R31, R34, R33, R35, R36, R37, R46, R134, R33, R150, R124, R151, R46 (intake and reading); 4b = R39, R40, R41, R38, R135 to R138 and R100 (doorbell, source protection, watches, redaction, the records request), run on the room 4a leaves behind (4b needs 4a's captured addresses, an uploaded reply and the second project member).

*Rows placed in this area when the areas were reconciled (merged or moved here, or recovered proposals), taken in the sitting where their prerequisites stand:* R136, R139, R140, R141, R142, R143, R144, R145, R146.

### Area 5 · Vouch; the people, organizations and ties the documents name

**The sitting.** One walker takes this area in one new empty room, after areas 1 to 3 have created the room's two accounts (administrator and member) and brought in one file of every kind. Fixtures needed before the first row: a freshly captured web page and an upload that are still held; a capture made "for" a second member's question, so the held row can say whose question it waited on; a wrong-year capture to set aside; a crucial document and a document in an unresolved contradiction (to read the "not eligible" reasons); a memo that names an agency, a vendor and a nonprofit; documents that name a person twice under one name and two people under one name; and an ordinance, an office and a followed Legistar body (the last comes from R41 in area 4, which belongs to the founder's later order; if it is absent, say so for the seats part of R59). Order: (1) R38 first, because everything after it needs vouched documents. Take the held list, the bulk vouch, the vouch on the document page, set aside and restore, then a second account for the asker's wait line and a second vouch (the member needed). R38 is listed in area 4 in map section 3; here it opens the area as the founder's journey does, and the walker of area 4 must not repeat it. (2) R55 next: resolve references to subjects on the just-vouched documents (accept recognised matches, testify a missed one), then register people, record facts from passages, aliases, same-person claims, and the corrections (withdraw, report a wrong match, expunge). (3) R56 organizations and the connections that find the memo naming them. (4) R57 the member's own ties, with the member account (the administrator then reads them). (5) R58 relations among others, which needs the people and documents of R55. (6) R59 last: offices, holders on a date, seats; it needs the places set up in area 1 and, for Legistar, R41. The new rows R158 to R149 sit after the rows they extend: R158 (connections on a document) after R56, R147 after R55, R148 to R149 after R55's corrections. Split with other areas: the vouch-to-cite-to-sign chain finishes in area 9 (R92); R38's ceremony ties step is R57's evidence only; the personal-fact boundary has steps in area 6 (R65) and area 9 (R94); the connection panel is shared with area 4 (document page) and area 6 (R64 Explore).

### Area 6 · Read, find, cite into a question

**The sitting.** One walker, one new empty room, administrator and one member. Before this area the earlier areas must have left: the room claimed with its people (the member account is needed for R152, the cross-project step of R50, a second decider in R157, and any second-account check of R46), a project the member is in, and documents of every kind brought in and vouched (area 3 and the held list), including a 20-page PDF (w10), the 1,354-page volume (0111), a scanned PDF, a text file, a CSV, a web page at an address whose text changes on every fetch (the walks used `httpbin.org/anything?walk=...`), and a watched address. Because citing needs an open question, the walker first opens ONE question in the project (R80's "open" act, `drawquestion`; R80 is written in area 9) and keeps it open for the whole sitting; a second question helps R49 (grade follows the read) and R50. Order: (1) R45 first, because it makes the legs that R47, R48 and R49 read: open a document, step through its pages to the last, select words, cite on the last page into the question (state whether the leg names the page or the whole document), cite a second passage and a web-page passage, then read the "already cited" line. (2) R46 Find, run from the Finder over everything now held (search, filters, one table, hold a set, frontier, 300+ hits, coverage words, "Find in" scopes); the 300-hit project and the late documents come from area 3. (3) R47 Find in this, on a document, on a held set and on a project, recording each kind of fact from a passage found. (4) R48 trust marks: read every mark on a document, declare origin, check the route, attest, and retire last (retire an uncited vouched copy; try the cited copy and read the refusal naming the question), because retiring changes what R49 and R50 can still reach. (5) R49: capture the changing page again AFTER the cited leg exists and wait for the sweep (it ticks every 1-5 min and rests about 55 min; a notice did not arrive within ten minutes in RW5 and RW6 when the cited paragraph was byte-identical, RW7/B.md:18), then adopt or keep from the Queue or from the question; this row shares the Queue with R21 and R22 (area 2) and the after-publication half belongs to area 9 (R92, R96). (6) R50 last: flag by hand on two documents that disagree, clarify, take up, dismiss a second one with its fixed reason, resolve; the cross-project steps (ask to resolve a conflict with a record you cannot see, respond) need the member as the other project's owner; publication's disclosure of an open contradiction is walked in area 9 (R92). The new rows R157 to R158 follow, R152 (needs the member) after R46 and R153/R158 after R45. Fixtures needed beyond earlier areas: two documents holding an amount that does not match (for R50), a pair that differs only by precision (the over-strictness arm), a second project holding a record the first member cannot see.

### Area 7 · Money, time, connections, registers

**The sitting.** One walker, in the new empty room, after areas 3 to 5 have left behind captured documents with cited passages (area 3-4), persons and organizations (area 5; the payer of the money table must be registered first, or the ingest writes 0 facts [RW7/B.md:12, PAYER_NOT_IDENTIFIED]) and a team time zone (area 1). Take the rows in the founder's order: R60 the timeline first (events from cited passages, participants, dated facts, relations, two lanes, undated items apart), then R157 the declared flow and its findings (instances are events threaded to steps, so the timeline must hold events; a Queue is needed to read the findings), then R62 tables and ingest as the **member** account (it is the only row of this area that needs the member; R157 needs a member only for the DEFINITION_MOVED race), then R61 the money trail (it reads the facts the ingest wrote, and a half-ingested table must show the partial marker meanwhile), then R63 analysis (two sources of one figure, a contract with change orders, an authority behind a payment: fixtures to prepare), then R64 Explore with Follow from here, then R158 connections made by hand (asserting a connection needs a document that names one thing twice; shared with areas 4-5), then R65 registers last (an import brings rows that Explore and the money trail then follow; watches and the outside alert need a filer, licence or docket named). R186 is split with area 4: it needs R50's contradiction to exist. Fixtures: an 18,098-row table in comma and in tab form, a figure held by two sources, a contract with change orders, an award with no solicitation (for the missing-predecessor finding), one Oakland NetFile / Form 700 / lobbyist / licence / CourtListener source per register. Walk order inside the money rows: ingest before trail before analysis. Registers are owed on T51 and may have no page (see notes).

### Area 8 · Law and courts

**The sitting.** One walker, administrator account, in the new empty room, after areas 1 to 7 have left behind the room (areas 1-2), the captured and vouched documents (area 3: a text file, a PDF, a web page, an OMC chapter page, a department policy, a court decision, a court register page), the document page's Find in this (area 4, R47, which is where "hold as a standard" is first pressed from a passage), and the persons and organizations that parties will be registered from (area 5). The founder order inside the area: (1) R70, capture the OMC chapter and hold its sections, ask what a section said on a date, relate it to a law; (2) R71, hold a standard from a text file, a PDF page and a web page, adopt a proposed one, then the "as heard" path (testify, confirming letter, dated fact, hold when confirmed in writing); (3) R72, a department policy with force per provision, "Cited, not seen", "Looked for, not found", history from two captures; (4) R73, a court decision held as a standard and related to the provision; (5) R74, a proceeding registered from a caption captured earlier, with a court chosen from Places (a court must exist or be added: "Add a court"), parties by role, orders held as obligations, follow. Nothing here needs the member account except confirming a rule (R105, area 10) and the member's own "as heard" account (testify); a second member is not needed for the core rows. Later areas use what this area leaves: R103 (area 10) needs a binding standard (it refused STANDARD_NOT_BINDING, RW2/B.md:54), R105 own rules and R100/R101 laws cite the held provisions; R80 (area 9 of the walkers' list) can rest a question on a law row. The extra rows R159 to R82 below were assigned to this area by helpers whose area numbers came from another numbering; they belong with "Question to conclusion" (map area 8 in section 3 of the draft) and are best taken after R80/R81 in that sitting. RW7 (build/plan/walk-T51-rewalk7/) walked none of R70-R74 (S33:47-133). No row of this area has any date of its own beyond the round causes listed.

### Area 9 · Question to conclusion (and the plan opened)

**The sitting.** One walker, one new empty room, taken after the documents, people, money and law areas have left material behind: a project with its bar set (R09, R24), at least ten captured and vouched documents of mixed kinds (a PDF and the web page it was captured from, so two citations share an upstream origin; a scanned page read by OCR; a page typed by hand by the administrator in R33; a held table or workbook from R36/R62 for the claim check; a city page that states a claim), the administrator and one joined member (a second member with a declared expertise for the "ask for a check" route, R12), and, for the cross-project acts, a second project that cites the same question and a hidden project. The walker starts with the Ask page without the assistant (R84), because it needs nothing; then opens the question (R80), answers the falsifier elicitation per citation and reads the shared-origin read-back (R80), cites supporting and cutting legs, reads the strength against the bar and changes the bar (R81), narrows, divides, holds a hunch and a hypothesis (R82), composes and moves reading versions (R165), concludes (with the reason, falsifier and project claim), then withdraws, reopens, severs and reinstates. Reminders to look again (R166) and the "which projects draw on this" check (R167) need the second project and are taken once it exists. The claim check (R83) comes after the tables of area 6 and uses the member for the spot-check visits; the second member's check (R85) needs the administrator's typed page from R33 and a second and third account. The plan is opened last, from a concluded question while the inquiry is still young (R102: open, matters, options, scenarios, checkpoints); its start-and-send and its close are walked in area 11. Two rows need material that only a later area makes: citing a published case as the basis of a new inquiry (R168) needs a published case (area 10), and citing another team's accepted finding (R169) needs the imported case of R98; put both at the end of the sitting or in a second short pass. Leave one hunch uncleared on purpose for the ceremony (area 10), which reads the refusal. Member needed: R80 (participant), R83 (spot-check visits), R85, R80, R166, R167, R169.

*Rows placed in this area when the areas were reconciled (merged or moved here, or recovered proposals), taken in the sitting where their prerequisites stand:* R159, R160, R161, R162, R163, R164.

### Area 10 · Case to publication (two sittings, 10a and 10b)

**The sitting.** One walker, in the new empty room, takes this area after areas 3 to 8 have left behind: a concluded question whose findings meet (or deliberately fall short of) the project's bar [DEC-72]; a vouched upload and a web capture with a copy, co-attested or not (area 3, 4; R38); a person with a career and a payee named (areas 5, 6); a standing hunch (area 8, R82) to provoke UNCLEARED_HUNCH; a watch on a source a case will cite (R41, R49, area 4); a second member who wrote an observation the case reaches (R57/R31 observation) and who is not an owner; an administrator who is not a project owner (bystander); and a second owner. Needed besides the administrator and the member account: a stranger browser (no sign-in) for `review-outside`, `published`, `docket-public` (frames `public`/`published`, registry `frames`); a team with a time zone (the walked room "biosmoke8" has one, RW7/B.md:11) and a second team with none (for the G62/G11 no-zone Sign step); and a published case from a second team for R98 (a second room, or the sample case of journey 25 [PL:59]; BOB to settle). Order: (1) R90 the case editor with several findings, a named person and payee, leaves-out, bias, the right-of-reply declaration and two drafts; as the observation's author (member) R171 attribution; R172 a photo in a finding; R182 two vouched captures of one source in one question beside a one-capture control. (2) R91 review copy: share with two outside readers, band and marker (R175), reader comments, member acknowledges, owner replies, export, stop one link. (3) R93 the signing key: register in the browser before the ceremony, then also load a key file, withdraw, and ask an owner to sign as a non-owner. (4) R92 the ceremony on a draft that has the refusals still standing: read every refusal first (uncleared hunch, unchosen credit, unchecked photo, below the bar, no finding, copy pending), repair each, reload before signing, each publishing member confirms her ties, then sign and publish edition 1. (5) R97 on a second prepared case (or edition): set a time, move it, cancel it, let one publish, make one held by a change; the no-zone Sign step on the second team. (6) The stranger's reading: R94, then R95 verify and recreate with the regenerated checker, R176 links out. (7) R98 in the second team: import, recreate, accept, withdraw, flag. (8) Last, because they alter what exists: R174 (the watched source changes, the findings are flagged, a new edition is warranted), then R96 (next edition with the What-changed statement and a fresh bias acknowledgement, the correction, a court order) with the docket rows R177 to R180, and R181 the withdrawal on a disposable case. The sitting is too long for one walker (21 rows, three accounts, a stranger, a second team, a set-time wait): **propose a split into 10a (steps 1 to 5: editor, attribution, photos, k = 2, review copy, key, ceremony, set-time publish) and 10b (steps 6 to 8: published case, verify, import, source change, docket, corrections, withdrawal)**. 10b starts from the published editions 10a leaves behind (one complete edition, one scheduled edition that went public, one case citing two captures of one source). The member account is needed in R90, R91, R92, R171, R172, R174, R177 to R181.

*Rows placed in this area when the areas were reconciled (merged or moved here, or recovered proposals), taken in the sitting where their prerequisites stand:* R170, R173, R178, R179, R183, R184, R185.

### Area 11 · Act

**The sitting.** One walker, in the new empty room, takes this area last, because almost every row needs material the earlier areas leave behind: a standard that binds the body being judged (area 7: R71, with the court or code rows R70/R73), an event on the timeline and documents to cite as evidence (areas 3 and 6), a concluded question or a published finding to rest the determination on (areas 8 and 9; a finding "checked and held", or another team's accepted finding, are the other two kinds [ACT:24]), the plan opened from a question in area 9 (R102 "Plan what to do"), a records office named or confirmed under Places (area 1/4, R100), and the member account (a second person is needed for the rule confirmation, the hold strip and a member's decline). Order, as a founder's real investigation runs: (1) **determination** (R103) on the matter page: pick the event, evidence and findings, record compliant / noncompliant / unclear per standard (the earlier walk met STANDARD_NOT_BINDING, so first make sure the standard binds the body); (2) **consequences and the escalation opened** (R104, first half), plus the member's decline to escalate (R187); (3) **breach action and filing**: the action itself (R101: kind, addressee refusals, laws, tier, confidential, reply), the filing template kept after a sent filing (R107, member), and the start-and-send from the plan opened in area 9 (R190 here; the row R102 itself belongs to its own area); (4) **due dates** (R106) with a past-dated fixture for the overdue mark and the plan checkpoint (R189); (5) **the other side's response**, evaluating it and advancing, holding or declining the next stage (R188); (6) **counsel and holds** (R105 and R191): mark a received reply as pressure or a legal threat, place the hold (member), prepare and send the notice, assemble the packet, draft a rule that a second member confirms; (7) **the matter closed** (R104, second half: success record and the refusals while compliance is not restored). A row that needs a later step says so: R106's overdue mark needs a dated action from step 3; R188 needs a recorded reply from step 3; R105's hold needs the legal-threat reply of step 5. R100 (records request round trip) and R102 are walked in this sitting as the way to produce the action but are scoped in their own area. Re-walk 7 (RW7) walked nothing in this area: RW7/A.md and RW7/B.md hold no Action, Matter, Counsel, Hold, Rules or Template row [S33].

*Rows placed in this area when the areas were reconciled (merged or moved here, or recovered proposals), taken in the sitting where their prerequisites stand:* R186, R192, R193, R194, R195, R196, R197, R198, R199.

### Area 12 · After the alpha (needs the assistant; not walked in the alpha)

**The sitting.** This area is not walked in the alpha; it is the walk of the first beta, after areas 1-11 have left a finished room. Everything the assistant needs must exist first: the team's account settings (area 1; the connect page and "not ready" state of R17, area 2), a project with an objective and a question with cited sources (areas 3, 9), a document with a captured page (area 4 sources), a case in progress (area 10) and a litigation hold (area 11) for the transcript row. Order for one walker: (1) R16, who pays (administrator opens `teamai`, then `projectai` as a project's owner; a project with a second member is needed to see the project's own sign-in cease to serve); then, as the member, mint an `ai` credential (R200 start); (2) R18 and the Ask prompt (R202), with the panel (R206) open beside it; (3) open a CHECK run over a concluded question and watch it (R200, R201); (4) extract proposals (R203); (5) suggestions and the suggestions switch with a second account (R205); (6) help-me-write and the other drafts (R204, R209); (7) tray and leads (R208, R210); (8) transcript and hold last, because it needs a published case (R207). Member account is needed for R200 (credential), R201 (teammate cannot read), R203, R205, R207. Rows named A12m-n are NOT walked here: they are the parts of the old R16/R18 that need no assistant and move to the earlier area named in the row (see Notes). Nothing in this area has a walk record; every row is NEVER.

*Rows placed in this area when the areas were reconciled (merged or moved here, or recovered proposals), taken in the sitting where their prerequisites stand:* R211.

### Overlay · for every walker

Citation shorthand used below: FR1/FR6/FR7 = the fix-round 1/6/7 plans (`P:` = line of the round-7 plan); IC = Interaction Constructs; RW7 = `build/plan/walk-T51-rewalk7/` (A.md, B.md, BRIEF.md); DR = Design Requirements; LT = `layouts.html`; PL = `lane-journeys/PLAN.md` (a plan, not a walk record); ARCH = Architecture; DI = Distribution; surfaceRules = `ux-experience.json`. Entries rest on the bundle; where a helper named a source only by its short id (T:, SRCH:, EXT:) it is kept as given.

##### The sitting

The overlay is not an area and has no sitting of its own: each walker carries the checks in part 1 through every screen of his or her own area and writes down, per screen, only what fails or what could not be forced. Three rows are different in kind. R108 (load) cannot start until the other areas have left a team with material in it (about 250 items in a project for the pickers, G20 [FR6 G20]); it needs five members signed in at once and a quiet copy for the re-time, so it is a separate sitting after area 10 (or the end of area 9 if area 10 is slow), taken from the member accounts the earlier areas created. The one-person run (R212) is not a second sitting: the walker tags in areas 1-10 every founder step that asked for a second account, and settles it at the end of area 10. The remaining rows (R213 to R217) are each done once, in the area named in the last column of the table's "what a walk must do" cell; they need the member account only where marked. Nothing in this area needs material from a later area except the published case (R213 band text, R170 print) which is area 9, and the records-request round trip (R216) which is area 10 (area 11 where the bundle's ADD list splits it).

#### 1. The overlay: checks every walker makes on every screen

Each check is made as the member as well as the administrator wherever the two differ. Record the screen, the check number and the words as rendered; a state that cannot be forced is recorded as "not forced", not as a pass.

1. **Undetermined.** One component, one fixed mark, a mandatory "because..." line, the same everywhere; never an error, never a zero. [surfaceRules statesToDesign.undetermined; DEC-86; IC section U, IC:654-682]
2. **Withheld.** A hidden object leaves its own treatment and one fixed sentence stating incompleteness, never identity, title, state or count. Test with the second account on every screen with a hidden object; downstream lists (dependents, cited-by) say something was withheld. [surfaceRules statesToDesign.withheld; DEC-86; DEC-36; openQuestions[22]]
3. **The other neighbours: Unrated, Nobody looked, Refused.** Each has its own visibly different treatment and fixed sentence pattern; none shares a look with Undetermined (the old UI used one badge for undetermined and "no grade yet"); none is dressed as an error; "Nobody looked" differs from looked-and-absent. Find each on at least three screens. [openQuestions[5], DEC-86, DEC-82; IC:654-682]
4. **Ordinary states.** Force an empty list ("Nothing here"), a slow read ("Still loading"), a failed read ("Could not read this, because...": a screen that cannot tell none from could-not-read must say could-not-read). Every wait carries one line naming what is awaited, from whom, and the date or "no date is set". A next step appears only on something just created (a new project: "Add the first question"). [DEC-98; DEC-94; openQuestions[14]; IC:654-682]
5. **Truncated.** Every list says when it was cut and at what stated bound (the plan's bound is 200 per page). [surfaceRules statesToDesign.truncated]
6. **A refusal teaches.** The plane's own words with offenders and arithmetic named, the shared standard sentence first, then the act's remedy or alternative; never "something went wrong", never a code, a detail or a raw id. The refusal is received, never computed by the UI; a refusal the plane would give is already stated before the press (pre-flight); a machine credential is not offered acts its class is refused. Record each refusal verbatim, as the member for administrators' acts. [DEC-83; DEC-49; IC:235-287; UI-KICKOFF:105-107; surfaceRules S[1] mustNeverShowOrDo]
7. **No prefill, no preselect.** No preselected choice, no prefilled, suggested or templated justification, no value entered under the member's name that she never saw. [surfaceRules S[1] mustNeverShowOrDo[0],[1],[2],[5]]
8. **Machine work is labelled** wherever it appears (agent-surfaced marker, "Suggested by the assistant", machine-prepared drafts), shown first with its reason and accepted in one attributed act; never a single confidence score, case-level strength, warning/error severity ladder, relevance score or rank figure. [surfaceRules S[1] mustShow[5]; across surfaces]
9. **Act weight by rung.** Reversible acts inline; reasoned acts show a reason field in place and refuse an empty one; terminal and attested acts open a full dialog saying what ends or cannot silently be undone and who signs; irreversible acts only through the ceremony (two named exceptions: the installer's short name and personexpunge, which have their own Irreversible dialog). Only acts heavier than Reversible show weight pips and the rung name; the scale sentence appears once, at the first marked explanation; no "are you sure" on a reversible or reasoned act; an act with no rung ruled is treated as reasoned; an act the caller's capability lacks is absent, not greyed; an act whose effect cannot be taken back outside the record (attributing a name, sharing a lead, naming a person in the registry, setting the team bar) opens the full dialog whatever its rung; the old "at X weight" wording is gone. [DEC-87, DEC-88, DEC-142, DEC-143, DEC-295, DEC-280, DEC-69; IC:684-751; surfaceRules S[1] mustShow[0]]
10. **The mark and the asking agree** (K4199, shell item T-07, CHANGE B17): an act marked as reasoned asks for its reason before it runs, never runs and then refuses for a missing reason; an act that runs on press (sign-in, own-account: enroll, knock, reauth, recover, setpassword, ask, infolevelset) carries no reasoned mark. Which release carries K4199 is unconfirmed. [FR1:211, FR1:284, FR1:10]
11. **Four button states.** Pressed (sinks, focus ring); working (turning ring, second press does nothing from press to answer, aria-busy, the `shell.working` live region says "Working...", data-done); answered (Terminal, Signed and Outward acts stay disabled with a check; others show a check about two seconds); refused (ready again, refusal beside it). Sounds (Your account > Language and appearance > Sounds): per member, off to start; on, a soft click on press and a low tone on a refusal. [DEC-224, DEC-280, DEC-295, DEC-299 (12); IC:733-751]
12. **Receipts, previews, set acts, conflicts.** Every act gives a receipt (what was written, by whom, when); a preview writes nothing; a set act says beforehand whether it is all-or-nothing, report-and-proceed or per-item; conflict states read as such: CAS_STALE names expected and got and is never merged, a 30-second lease held by another gives LEASE_HELD, a stale selection gives SET_MOVED or NO_SUCH_SELECTION. [surfaceRules S[1] mustShow[1],[3], statesToDesign]
13. **No nagging** (DEC-69): the product informs at the act once; repeating, re-confirming or measuring is the flaw; single and bulk forms are both available and neither forced. [DEC-69 lines 106, 129-139]
14. **Plain words and chips.** Every state has a plain reader-language meaning from one table; every chip is a click-over that discloses its row (what it means, enables, forbids and why, legal next states); the same state reads the same on every screen; "Stale" means different things per surface, so compare the sentence with that surface's own rule. [surfaceRules accessibilityAndLanguage; UI-KICKOFF:221-224]
15. **Voice and vocabulary.** "Team" not "group" (see Notes for BOB: the sources disagree on which governs), "your team's Civicsmith" (never "copy", except a review copy or a document's copy; "installation" only where hosting is the subject); "Status" for a condition, never "Signal"; "hint" for machine-judgement scores; never "secret" or "confidential"; American English; dates "October 8, 2026"; 12-hour clock; sizes in KB/MB; no raw id or raw time in member text; no AND, OR, disjunction, branch or ground label when structuring support (the walker records the rendered label, not the op name: `inquiryground`, "branches" in scenarios); no "overdue" on Home or in the Queue; no zero readings; tab titles read "{screen} · {team} · Civicsmith"; the one-line description of the product where there is room; never "civic groups" alone. [IC:820-842 (DEC-125, DEC-146, DEC-149, K3472); IC:119-121 (DEC-131); IC:887-889; DEC-224, DEC-280, DEC-295, DEC-299, DEC-316, DEC-340, DEC-379; DEC-412 (4); DEC-414 (1); surfaceRules S[1] accessibilityAndLanguage[4], DEC-32 clause 1]
16. **Orientation and explanations.** The path on every screen ends with the screen's own name; the rail drags from 184 px, moves by arrow keys, resets by double-click, goes icons-only under 120 px and is kept per member; resting the pointer 0.5 s on a mark, name, button or screen shows its explanation; focus and tap show it at once; Escape closes it; one element has one explanation (no explaining element inside another); a named record opens on click (a cited passage at that passage); two levels ("Explain promptly" / "On a longer pause") and the masthead switch with Alt+Shift+I; a term is explained the first time it is met, never pushed again, and findable in one list of every term; the cursor changes only over a control. [DEC-154, DEC-155, DEC-159 to DEC-163, DEC-174 to DEC-177; IC:977-992, IC:871-875]
17. **Phone at 390 px.** No sideways scroll; tables as labelled cards; tap targets at least 44 px (links in running text exempt); view-only is the stated MVP but everyday acts (reversible and reasoned, including capturing a photo as a member) must work; every Irreversible act, signing and sending is read there and says where to finish on a larger screen; five tabs (Home, Queue, Add, Find, More). [DEC-122, DEC-170, DEC-181; IC:809-812, 989, 1010-1011; LT:49-58, 133; surfaceRules accessibilityAndLanguage; PL:15-26]
18. **Light and dark.** Colours follow the device, the setting overrides; print and the complete edition are always light; colour is never the only signal (each grade scale has its own icon, each stage bar its name); contrast is AA. Dark mode is checked at least on Home, Project, Settings and a question. [DEC-122, DEC-79, DEC-82, DEC-170; IC:809-814; PL:15-26]
19. **WCAG 2.2 AA.** Operate the screen by keyboard alone; every hover explanation also opens on focus and tap; no keyboard trap in a panel or dialog; the assistant panel docks and never floats; every button has an accessible name; zoom to 200 per cent; with reduced motion on, the wizard ring does not move; state changes take 120-200 ms. [DEC-99; DEC-79; DEC-82; DEC-90; IC:781-787, IC:960-967; openQuestions[15]] (The level of screen-reader, keyboard and contrast support is listed as open in the surfaceRules, so record, do not fail: see 29.)
20. **Nothing from outside.** On member screens and the public case, no request leaves for outside fonts, analytics or trackers (typefaces, icons, scripts ship inside the team's copy); no email is sent anywhere; on a test copy the band above the masthead says "Test Civicsmith". [DEC-122; IC:809-814; DEC-224 and later]
21. **Role.** What a member lacks is absent, not refused or greyed, and refused by the operation layer if called anyway (needs the second account; made after R10 capability changes). [UI-KICKOFF:93-96; DEC-36; PL:15-26]
22. **The fence is spatial.** The member can tell working material from published material and a shared-for-review copy from another team's imported case by the band text (working, not public / shared for review / another team's case); the public sees no sign a fence exists. [UI-KICKOFF:86-92; LT:49-58]
23. **Identity is server-stamped.** No screen asks who you are; there is no way to act as another account (pairs with R04). [UI-KICKOFF:97-98]
24. **The tell** (DR-13): every act that makes an outward-visible request (asking a public archive to fetch a URL) says so at the point of the act. [UI-KICKOFF:99-101]
25. **Append-only shown as record.** History, session logs and release records read as record; nothing implies deletion where the system corrects by appending. [UI-KICKOFF:102-104]
26. **Alive.** A list open on one account updates, without disruption, when the other account adds or removes an item (needs both accounts; also R108). [UI-KICKOFF:207-209]
27. **Change weighs as much as state; layered.** What changed or is about to change is shown, with motion only where action is needed; the top level is simple and detail is on drill-down in plain language from the user's side. [UI-KICKOFF:193-198, 162-172]
28. **The wide path, per journey.** (1) a front door a newcomer can take alone; (2) wizards never gate; (3) the journey shows its stage and what comes next; (4) explained where it appears; (5) heavier steps slow you down; (6) any order, and unfinished work (a draft, a half-done wizard) is found as left after a reload and listed where the member will see it; (7) many front doors; (8) fast for experts (search, keyboard, acting on many). [DEC-129; IC:869-881]
29. **Open items: record, do not fail.** Wording of empty and loading states for every surface, accessibility level, languages other than English, how long captures and renders look while waiting, how a review-copy recipient learns of a change, RATE_IP/RATE_GLOBAL words. Stand-in words owed to the design stream (the 13 stand-in keys of H7 section 2 such as `reading-pipeline.index.bytes_not_held`; G44's `screen.open-file.safe.*` rows; `CASE_DOCUMENT_TOO_LARGE`; G48 sentences; G50 `case-grammar.casefile.<slug>` rows) are recorded as "open words". Looks are left to Design: check presence, wording and behaviour, not appearance. [surfaceRules across surfaces; DEC-91; FR6 G44-G50; H7 section 2; ux-experience.json openQuestions[27]]
30. **Not a security boundary.** No screen implies that projects are private from the team's administrators or from a compromised participant credential; any word about the root of trust says "whoever controls the hosting account". [ARCH:156-162, 652-653, 244-263, 1485-1487]
31. **Timing note on every screen.** Record the first mark and the answer time of each screen and act and whether a reading tick was running in the same object; a time taken while a tick runs is reported as that. The bars and the re-time are R108's. [FR7 P:309-310, 388; R108]
32. **Namespace discipline for any HTTP-driven step or fixture.** A call without `store=` lands in the real record for the ADMIN, MEMBER, DAEMON and PROBE classes; name `store=scratch` on every call and read the witness counters before and after. Operator acts (`op=livefire`, scratch `op=purge`, `aicredentialmint` with `confinedTo`, `op=audit`, release signing) are not room walks. [DI:116-120; D-456, D-461; DI:48, 92-135, 166-199; construct-status.json 1.namespace-*]
33. **What is not evidence or not walkable.** Old-app screens are not coverage of the new record types (K633, openQuestions[32]); a record older than the new room is not canon (SR:3, D-436); do not log designed-not-built acts as defects (see Notes for BOB, third group). [openQuestions[32]; SR:3]

Not in the redeploy: G83 (room moves to western North America for the copy; a California member's screens expected to lose 1-2 s each; PERFORMANCE re-times) [ADDED:62]; G88 (longest wait under 500 ms for the 12 MB text) [G88:29]; G84 (start-up margin; no member-visible change); G87 (Level 1 load figures on a scratch copy, outside T51, carried to next.md N1274; not a defect); G90 did not ship in redeploy 2496ff995b and is cut in release 0.87.0. RW7 did not walk G83/G84/G86/G87/G90 or the scheduled telemetry [RW7/BRIEF.md:15] | NEVER | (1) Five members signed in at once, each working in a different area, on a team that already holds material (about 250 items in a project so the pickers load, G20 [FR6 G20]). (2) Expected times to check: Project documents about 0.35 s (from 1.9 s); Held heavy rows about 0.5 s warm; Held with a project 1.5-2.5 s; Home 2.3-3 s; Timeline, Queue, Money 1.8-2.5 s; Project, Document, Ceremony 2.3-3 s; Question 3-4 s; a large capture answered under 2 s; floor about 2.1 s on today's hops; acts above 2 s are known and stated (conclude 3-4.5 s, prepare an edition 2.4-4.7 s, small web capture 7.4 s, uploads 10 MB 8 s, 23 MB 13-16 s, 40 MB 24 s); in-app navigation is measured first by the load lane; read compute as do-route minus in-route minus 115-160 ms ("object share 0%" proves nothing) [FR6 G12, G12e, G28, F6:108-120, 155]. (3) 12 and 24 concurrent `queue?count=true` with 24 `whoami` as the reference (5.6 s, 8.7 s, 1.3 s before G80) [FR7 P:284]. (6) Claim-serialized creation under load: a watch firing while a member captures the same address by hand yields one document, the loser records a yield and retries; a transient failure while the team's list is read creates no byte-identical second capture; when a site refuses every address each refusal is recorded per address, nothing is written, the due state stands [T:1478-1490, 1507-1520, 1322-1327]; chains of addresses stop at the first that yields (T:766-793). (7) Same-record writes by two members at once: see the two-writers rows of the areas that hold them (base-stamped write, divergence outcome). (8) G88: the 12 MB text's longest wait under 500 ms [G88:29]; first web capture (42.8 s) and the Document page (17-18 s) in RW7/B.md:24 are single observations to repeat. (9) Do not cite "84.8% of the window" as measured; it is undetermined between about 20% and 85% [SRCH:225-230, D-391]. (10) Record the `interfacewords` weight (6.3 MB per sign-in) and the Add screen's 37 `projectquestions` reads [RW7/B.md:23, B.md:25]. (11) A walk that cannot run five members at once records that, not a pass. |
